 This objective will be attained through: (i) awareness raising on the impact of climate change, and the generation of 
 resilient infrastructure, sustainable natural resource management, low carbon and climate-resilient agriculture, and the 
 development of adaptation strategies; (ii) access to energy from renewable sources. 
 The EDA Programme is built on the generated capacities of programmes executed by UICAR, and will contribute to the local 
 capacity building and community-based planning. 
 This objective will be attained through: (i) awareness raising on the impact of climate change, and the generation of 
 resilient infrastructure, sustainable natural resource management, low carbon and climate-resilient agriculture, and the 
 development of adaptation strategies; (ii) access to energy from renewable sources. 
 The Programme will be executed in a five-year period consisting of: 
 1. Organizational impact of climate change, and the generation of 
 mitigation strategies; (ii) the development of resilient 
 infrastructure, sustainable natural resource management, low carbon and climate- 
 resilient agriculture, and access to energy from renewable sources. 
 The lessons learned from the execution of rural development 
 projects will be used to develop and implement adaptation strategies that

 | the north Argentine Puna, in the extreme northeast of La Quiaca |

 | of 18°C (64°F). In winter, there are heavy rains and very low humidity, while in | 
 | summer. The daily changes in temperature are substantial, and conditions in the air, and there is diurnal warming up that re- |

 | winter dry period is intensifying and there is a prominciation | 
 | Climate change is gravely affecting the micro region's agriculture. The whole of the family works on the land | 
 | consumption. Only the remaining production is sold or exchanged. No forest lands, as trees are significantly scarce in the commu- |

 Type A family producers: These are small-scale family producers who make it difficult for them to broaden their production system and 
 Type B family producers: These are small-scale family producers who have a high level of knowledge about climate change 
 and its impacts on their livelihoods. They produce for themselves, their families or their communities. 
 Type C family producers: They are mostly producers in relation to the production of food crops and animals. They are usually organized into 
 consolidated associative forms. All these categories present Nils-type 
 characteristics. They are often members of associations, groups, associations and 
 cooperatives. Such associations may be formed by small-scale family producers who have a common interest in the production of food crops or animals.

<doc> For the monitoring of the counterpart of smallholders, there will be a 
 kind and cash counterparts made. The in-kind counterpart is usually estimated, according to the market value of 
 the products and services involved, and the cash counterpart is based on the actual costs incurred by the 
 Programme for the implementation of the project. The cash counterpart is then added to the 
 total amount of the counterpart to obtain the total cost of the programme. The in-kind counterpart is 
 included in the total cost of the programme and is subject to the same monitoring as the cash counterpart.

 ESUCO=C+

<doc> E.S.1. Existence of a national climate strategy and coherence with existing plans and policies, including NAMAs. 
 This report is based on the analysis of the National Climate Change Framework (NCCF) submitted by Argentina to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change in 2015. The 
 country’s identified priorities for low-emission and climate-resilient development, and the degree to which the activity is supported by a country’s enabling policy and 
 institutional framework, or includes policy or institutional charter, are summarized below. 
 The Republic of Argentina has actively taken part in the process of generation and development of the international Framework Convention on Climate Change. For several 
 years now, the country has implemented voluntary national climate change mitigation and adaptation actions in different sectors in 
 this regard. Argentina has already submitted three National Adaptation Plans (NAPs) and has recently sent its Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC) to the Convention every two years. This report was submitted in
